# Build log

Entries are added as things happen. Times are local (AEDT, UTC+11). Nothing here is tidied or written after the fact.

Sections used in entries: Work (start, merged, duration), AI error (what, how caught), Overruled (what, why), Parallel (what ran, result), Decision.

## Tuesday 27 October 2026

- 23:57 Work: planning session started. Read the earlier abandoned start (reference only, not copied). It has 10 behaviour rules (B1 to B10), 75 test cases in 8 files, and stub modules with no implementation. It was never committed.
- 23:58 Decision (fact check): the CFPB field reference says Product is "The type of product the consumer identified in the complaint". Issue is "The issue the consumer identified in the complaint". So the consumer sets these labels, not the CFPB. The labels are noisy and the README must say so. Source: https://cfpb.github.io/api/ccdb/fields.html (read 27 Oct 2026).

- 01:23 Cause of the earlier miss found: Claude wrote `.claude/settings.json` and ran the test in the same step, before Claude Code reloaded its settings. The docs (code.claude.com/docs/en/settings.md) say permission edits apply to a running session, and a docs agent confirmed the rule syntax. Retested after reload, all blocked: `git push --force --dry-run` (Bash, plain and with a `cd ... &&` prefix), the same from PowerShell, and `git push --dry-run origin main`. Lesson: test a guardrail only after it has loaded, and never trust it untested. Known limit from the docs: deny rules match command text, so forms like `git -C . push --force` are not caught. The T004 hook will check those.

- 09:00 Facts checked before T004, on the Claude Code hooks docs: a hook that crashes, fails to start or times out lets the action through unless `"onFailure": "block"` is set, which needs Claude Code v2.1.295 or later. This VS Code session runs extension 2.1.311. The `claude` command on the PATH is 2.1.252, too old for it. So every hook also maps any failure to exit code 2, which blocks on any version. gitleaks pre-commit form checked on its own v8.30.1 hook file: `gitleaks git --pre-commit --redact --staged`.

- 09:03 Decision by Claude (for owner review on the pull request): the test gate runs as a git pre-commit hook, not by parsing commit commands. Git builds the staged tree for every commit form (`-a`, `git commit <path>`, PowerShell), so the tests run on exactly what will be committed. The Claude hook only blocks ways around it and refuses to commit while the git hook is not installed. This also covers cut list items 2 and 4 (PowerShell commit parsing, rare commit forms) without building them.
- 09:05 AI error (caught by Claude in the red run): two new tests passed while red. Python itself exits 2 when a script is missing, so "bad input exits 2" passed with no hook. Red `ec784e8`: 72 new tests fail, the 58 old ones pass (`uv run --native-tls pytest -p no:cacheprovider -W error`).

It also ignored the design effect of an equal cap per Product. A worked example (one Product at 60 percent of the pool, 300 per Product) gave an effective size of 772 out of 3,000 for the re-weighted score. Pull request #7 turned back into a draft so it could not be merged with that rule.

The goal is a head-to-head contest: the LLM and TF-IDF label independently, each is scored against the recorded Product, and neither sees the other's label. (3) Only the Product level is labelled. Sub-products are counted as background only. Claude's error: it turned an earlier owner wish ("see how TF-IDF and LLM label in better precision") into a second claim the owner had not asked for.

The rule's inputs (smallest gap worth detecting, error rates, floor, split, ceiling) are for the owner to choose after the profile. Constitution 2.0.0, because principles I and III change.

- 15:22 AI error, found by reproducing it: the guard failures at 15:10 were not caused by backslash-u text. The hooks read their input in the Windows code page (cp932), not UTF-8. Fix planned in its own pull request: read the input as bytes and decode it as UTF-8.

- 23:27 AI error, found by the smoke test: two design gaps. (1) The recorded call keeps only the error type of a failed attempt, and the API backend's own message is a ModelError, so the real cause (for example a refused key, no credit, or an answer with no text) was hidden. (2) Every failure was retried 2 more times, including failures that cannot succeed on a retry, and the run went on after 5 complaints failed in a row. So most of the trial cap went on reservations for calls that probably cost nothing at the API. The installed SDK accepts both `output_config` and adaptive `thinking` (checked locally, no network), and the API docs list adaptive thinking with effort for Haiku 5.5.
- 23:40 The owner found no API token use in the API console. The likely cause is that the API organisation had no credit, so every call was refused and nothing was billed. Not confirmed, because the hidden message was lost. The owner topped up the API credit. The failed run's result files were moved out of the repo.

  - TF-IDF at the same coverage: re-weighted strict accuracy 79.1%, precision 81.2%, macro-F1 0.694.

  - Who was right: both 259, only the LLM 36, only TF-IDF 34, neither 41.
  - Quarantined: 7 `quote_not_in_text` (TF-IDF right on 6), 2 `unparseable` (TF-IDF right on 2).
  - Cost per 1,000 complaints in the run: LLM $0.2332, TF-IDF $0. LLM time per 1,000 not measured, because 63 calls were replayed. TF-IDF 0.239 seconds per 1,000, labelling only.
  - The figures match the 09:40 dry run. TF-IDF's time differs (0.407 then), as a time measurement does.
  - The row is the first in `EVAL_VERSIONS.md`. Results in `results/eval/v1_dev.csv` and `.json`.

- Facts checked before writing:
  - Microsoft's page "Deploy by using GitHub Actions" (dated 28 November 2025): the app registration, the service principal, the role "Website Contributor" scoped to the web app, the federated credential (issuer, subject, audience), and the three secrets.
  - GitHub's page "Events that trigger workflows": for `workflow_run`, `GITHUB_REF` is the default branch and `GITHUB_SHA` is the last commit on it. So the workflow checks out `workflow_run.head_sha`, the commit CI tested.
  - `azure/login` releases (`gh api`): v3.1.0 of 10 September 2026 is the latest, at commit `a641126d1b8aa4d1fa005f4f92df94a3a4c4c906`, with the same three inputs as the v2 on Microsoft's page.
- The deploy is a workflow of its own, not a job in `ci.yml`: a job skipped on every pull request would count as a failed check in the merge check.

The commands keep every ID in a variable and pass it straight to `gh secret set`, so none is printed. The repo's name is read with `gh repo view` at run time, so no file names the GitHub account.
- Not done by Claude: the Azure identity, the role, the federated credential and the three secrets. They are account and repo settings, so the owner sets them up. Until then the workflow fails at the sign-in step, and nothing is deployed.

Claude proposed fixes for each. They waited for the owner's go.

- The fixes for #56, tests first:
  - The job runs only for this repo's own pushes (`head_repository`). Its first step ends the run without failing while the secrets are not set, or when `main` has moved on since the tested commit (`gh api` with the run's own token).
  - The ZIP holds `BUILD_COMMIT`, through `git archive --add-virtual-file`. `/api/health` reports it as `commit`, only if it is a 40-character lowercase hex ID, else null. The run waits up to 10 minutes (20 tries of at most 15 seconds, 15 seconds apart) for the live app to report that exact commit. The manual redeploy script adds the same file.
  - The concurrency comment now says a newer waiting run replaces the waiting one.
  - `docs/DEPLOY.md`: the setup runs as one block that stops at the first failed command, adds the folders of `az` and `gh` to the PATH first, asks for the clone's folder with `Read-Host` instead of a placeholder, and sets each secret with `--body`. It says to run the block once, and what to do on a 403.
  - Spec 002 A15 and two known limits: Azure trusts any job on `main` that asks for a token, and the tip-of-main check runs when the deploy starts.
  - `test_A9_health_serves_the_counters` changed in green, with a comment: health also names the commit.
- Checked by hand before the commit: `git archive --add-virtual-file="BUILD_COMMIT:..."` with git 2.49 puts `BUILD_COMMIT` at the ZIP's root, holding the commit. PowerShell's parser finds no error in `scripts/deploy_azure.ps1` or in the guide's PowerShell blocks. The first parse found one, the old `cd <your clone of this repo>` placeholder.

  - Medium: five setup lines sat outside `& { }`, so a failed command there did not stop the rest, and a failed `cd` was never checked. `gh repo view` could then name another repo, and the credential and secrets would go there.
  - If a newer commit fails CI, the older green one is skipped too, and the skipped run shows as passed.
  - The setup tests did not check that every line is inside the block, or the folder and repo. `git rev-parse "origin/$Ref"` gives a tag object's ID for a tag. The 403 block had no checks, and `[0]` picked either of two registrations.
  - Log: the green time was missing, the first review's item 8 had no logged deferral, and two AI errors were not marked.
  - The reviewer ran `uv run` in the worktree. Claude checked afterwards: the worktree is unchanged and its environment imports `voc`.

Deferred with its reason, item 8: the log line with no verb stays, because log entries are not tidied.

- Claude's checks for the third option, from the Databricks docs: reading one volume needs USE CATALOG, USE SCHEMA and READ VOLUME, so a grant on one volume only is possible. A workspace admin can make a service principal's OAuth secret in the workspace settings, with no account console. Workspace users get USE CATALOG on the workspace catalog and USE SCHEMA and create rights on `default` by default, but no READ VOLUME or SELECT. Whether the Files API needs the workspace-access entitlement is not documented, so pull request 5 tests it. A test with the reader's credential can expect refusals from calls that return names or metadata at most, so it cannot leak text. Claude chose the third option, with the results repo as the fallback if pull request 5's checks fail.
- Claude's checks for the approval rule: `gh api .../actions/permissions/workflow` gives a read-only default token and Actions unable to approve pull requests. The deny rules block `gh pr merge` but not a merge through `gh api`. That gap goes into pull request 2 (J14).

Claude then blamed the laptop's software that inspects web traffic, the cause of uv's `--native-tls`. At 20:23 the same check from Cloud Shell gave the same HTTP 0.

At 20:35, with 5 seconds between calls, the same, and Claude dropped the concurrency idea too early, since 5 seconds was too short to test it. Claude then read it as instances stopping after each call. The logbook's count by instance at 20:48 showed one instance at a time, each living 2 to 3 minutes.

- AI mistake: the plan of 5 November set the maximum instance count but not the HTTP concurrency, and its research missed that Python apps take one call at a time by default. Caught by the live test.
- 21:00 The owner set the HTTP concurrency to 4 per instance, by hand, with the maximum instance count still 1. Three calls in a row from Cloud Shell: 401 in 2.0, 0.12 and 0.07 seconds.

So the queue function stored the message 9 seconds after it was taken.

### Pull request: CI runs once per change and retries its downloads

- Work start, 21:57: branch `fix/ci-downloads`, worktree `../voc-demo-ci`, from main at `fe61204` (owner decision, option A).

- The change, in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`:
  - CI runs on every pull request and on pushes to `main` only. A pull request's push ran CI twice before, once for the push and once for the pull request. The dashboard's deploy follows CI's push run on `main` (A15), so `main` keeps it.
  - The `tests` and `repo-checks` jobs install Python and the project with `uv sync --locked` first, up to 3 tries with 20 seconds between them. `repo-checks` had no install step, so its first `uv run` downloaded Python, where the 504 of 9 November hit.

- Spec 001's B17 says CI runs on every pull request and every push to `main`.
- Not covered: the `setup-uv` and `checkout` actions download too, and this change adds no retry around them. A fault there still needs the owner's re-run.
